<page-num>162</page-num>
->### SPECULATIVE PHILOSOPHY<-

...treasure <note>original: *thesaurum*; completing the word from the previous page</note> exists as the greatest, and it is not outside of himself. From within him proceeds (though indirectly) that by which he acts upon the external world, producing what is seen with the eyes. Therefore, unless a person is blind in mind, he will see—that is, understand—who and what he is internally; by the light of nature, he shall know himself through these external manifestations. 

It is certain that from the blood of man is generated a **natural Salt**, the balsam <note>balsam: in alchemy, this refers to the internal preservative fluid or life-force that prevents the body from decaying</note> of his body, which contains within itself both corruption and the preservation from corruption. For in the nature of things, there is nothing that does not contain within itself as much evil as it does good. 

Likewise, from the bones and flesh is generated **natural sulfur**, which is ignited due to the drying out of the body’s natural moisture <note>original: *liquoris naturalis*; the vital fluids that keep the "sulfur" of the body stable</note>. This fire sometimes consumes both the whole body and the sulfur itself. The cause of this fire is that man was, from the beginning, made of sulfur; this was then transmuted into flesh and blood, which are the **middle matter** <term>materia media: the intermediate state of substance that makes up the living human body, sitting between its primal elements and its final decay</term> of man. The "first nature" or "first matter" hates this middle state greatly and strives to reduce it back to itself. When it has succeeded, earth and ash remain—the remnant of the "first matter" of man and the subject of his "ultimate matter" <note>ultimate matter: the final state of a substance after it has undergone its full cycle of change, such as ash after fire</note>. 

Because man is generated in a state of corruption <note>a reference to the "fall of man" or the inherent mortality of biological life</note>, his own proper substance pursues him with hatred. From this it follows that the natural sulfur of man recedes from the "middle creation" and returns toward the "first." As it approaches that state and assumes its nature, it passes into an **elemental operation**. This elemental fire is indeed the cause of decay, and it is ignited by the **invisible sun** <note>invisible sun: a central concept in Hermetic philosophy representing the spiritual or archetypal source of all heat and life, distinct from the physical sun in the sky</note>, which remains unknown to most people.

**Terms to Know:**
*Natural Salt, balsam, sulfur, corruption, first matter, elemental fire, invisible sun*

-># PHILOSOPHY<-

This is the **sun of the philosophers**. There are such virtues <note>original: *virtutes*; referring to inherent powers or properties</note> in man that, through the power of corruption, they are able to dry out the bodily fluids; in this way, they reduce the salts of man into the nature of lime <note>original: *calcis*; referring to calx, the crumbly powder left after a substance has been burned or oxidized</note>, through which the body passes into a sulfurous state of being. Therefore, the body is reduced into **first matter** <term>materia prima: the fundamental, undifferentiated essence of all substance</term>, which is the state closest to being ready to receive the fire that breaks man down into his original essence. 

We see this same process in other creations, such as in the earth or the mountains. And although the bodies themselves are not the same, the operations are identical: the fire is not the same in its accidental properties <note>accidental: in Aristotelian philosophy, "accidents" are qualities like color or heat that don't change the core essence</note>, but it is the same in its fundamental substance. The Sun is invisible within men, whereas in the earth it is visible; nevertheless, both proceed from one and the same Sun. Man is indeed the fuel, in which—by means of the whetstone, namely <term>Mercury</term>, and the steel, namely <term>Heaven</term>—the sparks of this kind of fire are struck, find nourishment, and exert their powers.

The <term>Archeus</term> <note>the "internal chemist" or governing vital force that directs the body’s functions</note> naturally practices the chemical art within man, just as a skilled <term>Chemist</term> acts as a refiner of minerals in his forge. He separates three things—namely, chemical <term>realgar</term> <note>originally a red arsenic mineral; here used as a specific alchemical principle of the body</note> and Gold—from the raw ore. For as long as these remain joined together, each has no power by itself; but when they are separated, each expresses its own active powers within the man. Such is the "horizon" within him—not in his own strength, but in the perfection of his creation. Thus, the natural art acts upon the <term>microcosm</term> <note>microcosm: man viewed as a miniature representation of the entire universe</note> so that the man may be separated into "gold" by the action of internal lightning. In its proper place, this process separates the Chemical part into <term>loppa</term> <note>dross, slag, or waste material left over from smelting</note> and the realgar into smoke, vapor, or...

<page-num>164</page-num>
# SPECULATIVE PHILOSOPHY

...a sublimated body: which the one called the **Archeus** perfects in man through the arts of Vulcan <note>referring to alchemy and the transformative power of heat</note>, until it is brought to its ultimate matter. For it is the ultimate matter of things, which rejoices in its exaltation <term>exaltation: an alchemical process that raises a substance to a higher state of purity or power</term>, just as gold—separated from the other two <note>likely referring to the other two "Prime Principles," Salt and Sulfur</note>—glories in the fire, as does glass.

Nor can I remain silent about what our teacher, **Theophrastus Paracelsus**, wrote further concerning the secrets of the terrestrial Sun <note>gold</note>, specifically the difference between the **quintessence** of gold, **potable gold**, and the **oil** of the same. For the quintessence of the Sun is a tincture extracted by the spirit of wine <note>concentrated alcohol</note>, the method for which we have set down above. But **potable gold** is when the gold is dissolved into a potion together with other spirits and liquids. Yet the **oil of gold** is when a pure oil is produced from the substance of gold alone, by itself.

->Description of Potable Gold<-

Take one ounce of gold leaf or powdered gold; let this be dissolved into a juice. Add as much distilled vinegar as is sufficient; distill it afterwards and separate the components from one another. Repeat this process until no flavor of the additives remains. Next, let all of it together be immersed in five ounces of *aqua vitae* <term>aqua vitae: "water of life," a term for highly rectified alcohol</term>, and let them be digested in a **pelican** <note>a specialized alchemical vessel with two side-arms that allow the liquid to circulate and "digest" continuously</note> for a month.

->The description of that *aqua vitae* is as follows:<-

Take ten pounds of "burning wine" <note>distilled spirit or brandy</note>, and one handful each of roses, lemon balm, rosemary blossoms <note>original: *roris marini anthos*</note>, wallflower <note>original: *cheiri*</note>, the leaves of both kinds of hellebore <note>referring to black and white hellebore, used in various historical medicinal preparations</note>, and marjoram.

...one <note>continuing the list of ingredients from the previous page</note>, two ounces each of cinnamon, mace, nutmeg, cloves, herb-paris berries, all the various peppers, and cubebs; six ounces each of the juice of **celandine** <note>original: "chelidoniæ"</note>, mullein, and lemon balm; and five ounces of bean ashes. Finally, separate the components, and the result will be reserved for the use we have described above.

### *Description of the Oil of the Sun.* <note>original: "Olei Solis"; in alchemy, the Sun represents gold</note>

After the gold has been reduced into a juice by means of vinegar, as was stated above, let it boil for fifteen days in the following digestion. Afterward, let the volatile part be separated in a **bain-marie** <term>balneo Mariae: "Mary’s Bath," a water bath used to provide gentle, even heat</term>; the pure, fixed oil of gold will remain at the bottom.

### *This is the body for the digestion.*

Take one pound of celandine juice and an equal part (or slightly more) of circulated *aqua vitae* <term>aqua vitae: "water of life," a term for highly concentrated distilled alcohol</term>. Let these be resolved into a spirit, with which you should mix three ounces of gold; then proceed as was stated above.

### *Conclusion of the Philosophical Key.*

*Having previously provided the Key, I shall not be at all reluctant to soon hand over its use and the "chests" <note>arcas: likely referring to the repositories of secret knowledge or the vessels containing the substances</note> for the dissolution of these substances and their philosophical separation from their contents to any diligent searchers of nature's secret cabinets. To them—through this work, beyond all my other various labors, and by means of a certain supernatural artifice that elucidates the said Key—I shall prove that I have lived more for others than for myself up to this point: because...*

<header>MIRROR OF PHILOSOPHY <note>original: "Speculum Philosophicum"</note></header>

Indeed, the matter itself teaches this, and all who have seen me will bear witness to it. It would be fair, therefore, for me to rest for a while until at last another of our profession might step forward to lighten the burden. I do not say this because I yield <note>the original "edã" is likely a transcription of "cedam" (to yield or give up)</note>, but rather so that I may catch my breath to recover my strength against those attempting—albeit in vain—to defeat the truth of nature which Paracelsus <note>Theophrastus Paracelsus (1493–1541), a revolutionary physician and alchemist who championed the use of chemicals in medicine over traditional Greek authorities</note> professes. Nor do I see much leisure for myself, for that great leader of his enemies is close at hand. I must rise up, for the sound of weapons echoes from both sides. Yet, with my pebble and sling <note>a biblical allusion to David’s victory over the giant Goliath (1 Samuel 17), suggesting that simple truth can overcome a massive, arrogant opponent</note> already prepared, I do not fear this giant, even if I appear as a pygmy to him. For Truth arms her own against men who are liars, ambitious, and proud—those who hide under a cloak of eloquence because they would never dare to step out naked into the light with simple speech, lest the vice hidden within their "adorned tombs" <note>an allusion to "whitewashed tombs" (Matthew 23:27), appearing beautiful externally but containing corruption within</note> be revealed. In the power of Truth, therefore, I come to the encounter. Fare as well as you can.

<page-num>167</page-num>

## ON THE SUPERNATURAL
## ART.

It is fitting for us to prove from the very start that our supernatural art is the legitimate offspring, and that any other *Chemical* arts are illegitimate. *Modern Physicists and Metaphysicians* <note>The author is targeting the university-trained scholars of the 17th century who followed Aristotle and often dismissed alchemical claims</note> stubbornly deny our quintessence <note>literally "fifth essence"; in alchemy, this is the purest substance, a celestial medicine that exists beyond the four earthly elements of fire, air, water, and earth</note>. This is the best argument for us against such men, to show that their opinions amount to nothing. What do they judge the heavens to be? Will they dare to deny to us that it is a *fifth kind of being*, distinct from the four elements—separated by God as the pure from the impure—or will they confess their ignorance of this very thing? There is no doubt that they do not understand this secret <note>original: "arcanum," a term used by alchemists to describe a deep, hidden mystery or a potent medicinal substance</note>. 

If they admit to us that the heavens are separated from the first four essences, then it is indeed a *quintessence*. Unless, perhaps, they say the heavens are also made of the four *elements* and four pure *essences*, making eight essences in total. By this very claim, they would deny that the heavens exist in a pure state, and they would say they are just like the *elements* here below. For in this context, purity is defined more by its unity <note>the idea that the "quintessence" is a single, perfectly balanced substance, unlike the "mixed" and warring elements of the earth</note> than by its simple clarity. If the four *elements* were in the heavens mixed together as they are below, they would appear mixed there just as they do here—which is not the case at all. Instead, it appears to be a single *element*, adorned with many celestial bodies, as many as there are in the lower world, which correspond to them as effects to their cause <note>a reference to the Hermetic maxim "as above, so below," suggesting that things on earth are reflections of celestial realities</note>. This is the true separation of the *elements*, which true *Alchemists* ought to imitate: namely, the separation of the purer substance from the impure *elements*, just as the higher is separated from the lower. Common separation...

<summary>The author argues for the legitimacy of alchemy by comparing the "quintessence" to the substance of the heavens, claiming that just as the celestial realm is a pure fifth element separated from the four earthly ones, the alchemist must learn to extract pure substance from impure matter.</summary>

<keywords>quintessence, four elements, celestial bodies, arcanum, alchemical separation, metaphysics, purity, cosmology</keywords>

<page-num>168</page-num>

-> # PREFACE <-

*...this separation—that which occurs when oils are removed from waters, and these from their respective earths—belongs to the realm of qualities, not to the elements themselves. For example, if oil (which some call "air" <note>In certain alchemical traditions, "oil" was associated with the element of Air because of its ability to carry "spirit" or life-force</note>) is separated from its earth and water, nothing else has happened except that the elements—which were more viscous, more oily, or hotter—have been produced from their own qualities through this separation. Indeed, every element is present in every one of these parts, no matter how they are separated. It is therefore more sound to name them as follows: the **hot element**, for the part to which fire and heat most closely adhere; the **moist**, for that where water and moisture predominate; the **cold**, for that where the cold of air resides; and the **dry**, for that where the dryness of earth is most found. We shall also make use of these names below.*

*But to return to the heavens: the ancient Sages affirmed that this is the incorruptible part of the world, which they called the **Superior World**. They declared that it was separated by God from the other, lower part of the whole—namely, the corruptible **Inferior World**. We most aptly call this superior part the <term>Universal Quintessence</term> <note>original: "vniuersalem essentiam quintam"; the fifth element, believed to be the pure substance of the stars and the goal of the Great Work</note> and the **Natural Form**. The Inferior World, however, we call the **Universal Matter of Nature**, which is capable of receiving all its various forms. Before these two parts we have mentioned were separated from their whole (which is called <term>Chaos</term> <note>In alchemy, "Chaos" refers to the "First Matter" (materia prima)—the formless, undifferentiated state of all things before they are refined</note>), each contained the other in a confused state. Thus, even after their separation, each retains a primary bond of union and love within itself, each part desiring daily to be connected back to the other. Indeed, this affection and desire is the cause of all <term>generation</term> <note>original: "generationis"; the process of birth, growth, and the creation of new substances</note> and is truly called **Nature**. These are the principles of our **Physics** <note>original: "Physices"; at this time, "physics" referred to the study of all natural phenomena and the soul of the world, rather than just mechanics</note>, which in my previous...*

<meta>catchword: ...tibus [continuing "præcedentibus"]</meta>

...have been treated in the preceding booklets. Now, let us truly examine the principles of our supernatural artifice, so that those who so sharply and incorrectly attack it cannot claim it lacks a foundation. Because this is properly nothing other than an art consisting of natural principles, yet surpassing nature through artifice—or rather, through a certain divine mystery—it was necessary to use the same principles as nature herself, for we are unable to manufacture any others. 

But where nature has ended her <term>Physics</term> <note>original: "Physicam"; referring here to the study of the visible, material world and its natural processes</note>, we begin our <term>Metaphysics</term> <note>original: "Metaphysicam"; the author uses this term to mean "beyond physics," referring to the alchemical art that perfects what nature leaves unfinished</note>: taking those things which she has left to us as naturally finished and perfected, we further prepare and more than perfect them through a certain supernatural artifice. 

And because we cannot grasp the entire great heaven of nature with our hands, nor enclose all the elements within our instruments, we are compelled—in likeness and imitation of nature—to hunt for our own "heaven" from natural things by our mastery of separation. We separate our heaven from our impure elements so that we may fashion our own <term>Chaos</term> <note>original: "Chaos"; in this context, the "First Matter" or the raw, disorganized state of matter before it is purified and reorganized by the alchemist</note>: from which we must begin this admirable work, which ought to excel even the mastery of nature. 

Now, if I am not mistaken, you will understand why we say that our metaphysics surpasses every work of nature. For the separation of the heavens from the elements, or the first division of Chaos, was not a natural artifice, but a purely divine one. Yet in this we are truly said to imitate nature, because we work in this art by way of <term>corruption and generation</term> <note>original: "per modum corruptionis atque generationis"; the natural cycle of breakdown and rebirth or growth</note>, and we use the same principles as she does. More broadly, we say that in any natural body composed of elements, the heaven and the elements consist in part...

<meta>This page continues the author's argument that the alchemical process mirrors the divine creation. Having previously mentioned the "separation of our heaven," the text now explains how this laboratory separation creates a "microcosm" or "small world" that can produce a universal cure.</meta>

<page-num>170</page-num>

-> ### PREFACE ### <-

...be contained: otherwise they cannot be generated. Therefore, while we separate these things by this method, we are able to possess form and matter similar to those things from which <term>Chaos</term> <note>original: "Chaos"; the primal, unformed state of the universe. The author argues the alchemist must return matter to this state before "re-creating" it.</note> existed before its own division. 

Why, then, does <term>Cerberus</term> <note>original: "Cerberus"; the multi-headed guard dog of the underworld, here representing the author's fierce and stubborn critics.</note> still bark so wrongly at us? As if the things taught through this art were impossible! If the higher heaven and the lower elements generate all things through their own nature, why—using parts of that same heaven and those same elements—should a **microcosm** <note>original: "paruus mundus"; literally "small world." The belief that the human being (or an alchemical work) is a miniature map of the entire universe.</note> not be generated through us, when their "middle nature" is enclosed by us within our own instruments, through a supernatural mastery similar to the first division of Chaos? This is possible if God, however, grants his favor to anyone; for without Him, no end is perfected, even if it was begun correctly. 

This is the only way by which one may arrive at the **universal and metaphysical medicine**, which cures all diseases without distinction—a goal of which modern physicians despair. Do they not see that any particular thing is included within the universal? Since, therefore, such a medicine is fashioned from the incorruptible heaven and incorruptible elements (or those reduced to incorruptibility), and fashioned not only in the likeness of the universal world but even of the **resurrection**, why will it not prevail against all the corruptions of the microcosm that relates itself to this medicine? 

If those <term>Zoili</term> <note>original: "Zoili"; a classical term for petty, envious, or carping critics, named after the ancient Greek critic Zoilus.</note> understood the mystery of the **resurrection**, perhaps they would not dissent from metaphysical truth. But because they follow the dreams of those who were ignorant of the resurrection and denied it, they are utterly incapable of understanding this secret, nor are they worthy of it. What is it to us, however much more they try to resist the truth?

<summary>The author defends the possibility of creating a 'universal medicine' by mimicking God's division of Chaos within a laboratory setting. He argues that this medicine works because it reflects the incorruptible nature of the heavens and the mystery of the resurrection, despite the skepticism of modern physicians.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s defense of alchemy against critics (referred to on the previous page as "Cerberus"). The author shifts from defending the possibility of the "universal medicine" to the practical theory of extracting the "Fifth Essence" from natural substances, transitioning from abstract philosophy to laboratory instruction.</meta>

<page-num>171</page-num>
<header>P R A E F A T I O. 171</header>

...they will reveal their own ignorance and the crudeness of their talent, or the darkness and blindness of their mind, all the more nakedly. Let us turn ourselves toward the students of the true arts, and let us ask God to remove the clouds from the eyes of others, so that they may be able to see those things which He wishes to be revealed to His own. 

Having previously laid the foundations and set down the principles in our unique <term>Chaos</term> <note>original: "Chaos"; as mentioned on the previous page, this refers to the primal, unformed state of matter that the alchemist seeks to replicate in the laboratory.</note>, it will be worthwhile to teach the **extraction of its parts from natural compounds** to those who believe that this <term>metaphysical work</term> <note>original: "opus metaphysicum"; a labor that transcends physical nature to achieve spiritual or perfected results.</note> was given by God to men of good will. We have said, of course, that these parts are the **heaven and the elements of all things**. It is certain that these are naturally present in all things produced by nature, but in some more, and in others less; in some, they are even easier to extract than in others. 

Hence, it is the opinion of the philosophers that those things which are nearer and easier are to be more readily embraced. Such things include: **excellent wine, Tartar** <note>original: "Tartarum"; the potassium bitartrate crust left in wine barrels, highly valued by alchemists for its "fiery" properties.</note>, **fresh Honey, Flower of Rosemary** <note>original: "Flos anthos"; from the Greek "anthos" meaning flower, often specifically referring to rosemary in early pharmacy.</note>, **red Lily, Celandine, Mercury-herb**, etc. From these, either by themselves or by other means, the <term>fifth essence</term> <note>original: "essentia quinta" or quintessence; the purest, medicinal soul of a substance, separated from the four physical elements of earth, air, fire, and water.</note> can very easily be separated from its coarser elements, and these can be redistributed into their predominant qualities, while the individual elements remain within any given element. 

It should also be noted that in this context, <term>philosophical wine</term> <note>original: "vinum philosophicum"; not necessarily beverage wine, but a specialized alchemical solvent or any liquid from which a "spirit" or quintessence can be drawn.</note> can refer to any liquid from which its fifth essence can be separated through artifice, as we will teach below regarding **wheat, bread, grains, or juniper berries**, etc. Nor should you be unaware that, if you wish to be a practitioner in this art, **form and matter**,

...[of those] attacking <note>The text continues from the previous page's discussion of those who criticize what they do not understand.</note> disciples, who, because they have not yet seen the light, can neither bear it nor wish others to see it. Even while such a person cannot prove these slanders (however learned he may be), he compares honest men to obscure "ape-ists" <note>original: "simiistis"; a mocking pun coined by the author, blending "simia" (ape) and "chemist" to describe those who merely mimic the motions of alchemy without understanding the science.</note>—if I may call them so—rather than chemists <note>original: "chymistis"</note>, who like apes counterfeit any work. It is not enough to say, "this man is a scoundrel and a deceiver" <note>original: "deceptor"</note>: rather, he who dares to call others such things must bring forward the reason why he is so; otherwise, he openly shows himself to be such a person by this very act.

For it is the Devil’s way to call any others slanderers, because he would wish everyone to be so; thus also his sons persecute the good. Yet some who resemble chemists have also wished to refute true chemists, perhaps because they used different words than the ancient true chemists established, or because they attributed this art allegorically to the fabulous fictions of poets—by which figure of Rhetoric <note>original: "Rhetorices figura"; the art of persuasive speaking or writing.</note> it is very easy to twist anything to another and false meaning. But if you judge their writings correctly, you will see that neither side understands the other, or that both have not yet hit the mark. 

Where does envy <note>original: "inuidia"</note> or ambition <note>original: "ambitio"</note> not reign? Many—either because they cannot master this art, or because they see others with a more successful talent for it than themselves—attack <note>The text ends mid-word on the catchword "inuehuntur," meaning "they rail against" or "attack."</note> either the art itself or those who have written more obscurely than their intellect can bear.

# P R E F A C E. G.

...are distinguished <note>The text completes the word *separantur* from the previous page, likely referring to how true practitioners are distinguished from imitators.</note>: and by this they think they have earned praise for themselves, because they have carped at others. Yet they do not write what the truer goal <term>scopus</term> <note>original: "scopus"; the ultimate aim or target of the alchemical work.</note> of the art might be, nor do they explain it more lucidly or clearly. As for all those men of this sort, along with their biting "files" <note>original: "mordacibus limis"; a metaphor for abrasive, eroding criticism.</note>, I hardly value them at a straw. If I am able to bring anything of value to this art, or to treat it in clearer words—while still maintaining my modesty—I will diligently do what is fitting for one man toward another out of charity. 

When they attack me, I shall see how and by what reason I will respond. I am not inclined to move against others willingly until I have been provoked by their words, deeds, or writings (as has happened before, falsely and wrongly, to the harm of the perpetrator and his own disgrace). These are the matters, therefore, which I set forth for pious and honest readers to read first, and then to compare most exactly with the writings of the ancients; where they see that they agree, let them place their faith; otherwise, let them not. 

Yet it would be impossible for even the most outstanding intellect to learn the matter itself by reading alone. Therefore, it is necessary for anyone who wishes to achieve his desire in this art to become a master through practical work <note>original: "operando"; emphasizing that alchemy is a "hands-on" experimental science, not merely a theoretical one.</note>. Writing can be compared to nothing better than a key: unless this key is handed over, easy access to locked things is not open, nor can it be unless it is inserted into the lock. Likewise, until you have applied your hands to this work, you will not understand the secrets <note>original: "arcana"</note> which God has enclosed within the nature of things so that they might eventually become known to men. 

Therefore, we propose these things to you so that you may receive the key, by which you may learn—through sincere study—to unlock the physical locks by means of metaphysical mastery <note>The author suggests that understanding the spiritual or "metaphysical" principles of creation is the master key to manipulating "physical" matter.</note>. Meanwhile, whatever labors we have undertaken, we have offered them for your sake, honest *Reader*. Take them in good part and farewell.

->On metaphysical separation performed by itself.<-

### CHAPTER I.

We stated previously in the Preface that heaven is the fifth element—not because there are five elements, but only four. Their purer substance was supernaturally divided by God and brought back into a state of union in heaven; the more impure part remained below, divided into four parts that constitute the lower, elementary world, while the other part constitutes the ethereal world. No differently is our <term>metaphysical fifth essence</term> <note>original: "essentia quinta"; also known as the quintessence, the purest and most refined core of a substance that transcends the four physical elements.</note> drawn from the more impure elements of things by a supernatural mastery through separation. 

This separation is not what many have thought—those who deviate from the truth and falsely believe that the four elements can be separated so completely that one is entirely free from the others. For if this were the case, the <term>machine of the world</term> <note>original: "machina mundi"; a common philosophical metaphor describing the universe as a vast, divinely-ordered mechanism.</note> would easily be dissolved. But to prevent this from happening, the Most High Creator of all <note>original: "Altissimus omnium Conditor"</note> provided for it, willing that these four parts be preserved under such a union that, although one part can be distinguished from the others by its qualities, it nevertheless retains their essences within itself. 

He revealed this union to us more clearly in heaven, where no distinction of parts is visible; rather, He enclosed unity under the bond of superior and inferior nature, which are the same and a single nature. Therefore, there is a double separation: namely, one of the elements, such as the purer from the more impure; and another of the elemental qualities. This latter type is common <note>original: "vulgaris"; referring to standard chemical or physical changes.</note>, but the former is truly metaphysical. Nature, however, operates through only

This occurs in certain bodies by itself, as in wine, and in others through a <term>vehicle</term> <note>original: "vehiculum"; an intermediary substance, such as a solvent or carrier, used to extract an essence that cannot be drawn out on its own.</note>, as in grains and seeds. 

Although the <term>fifth essence</term> <note>original: "essentia quinta"; the quintessence, or the purest, most refined spiritual core of a substance.</note> lies hidden in all bodies produced by nature, in none is it so abundant as in wine, and from no other thing can it be so easily separated. For this reason, the <term>philosophers</term> <note>original: "philosophi"; meaning the alchemists or "philosophers by fire."</note> preferred wine, because of the ease of its separation—not because the fifth essence is inherently better or more excellent in itself than that drawn from any other body. For that celestial virtue is universal to all things and is equal; it only appears different because of the diversity of the <term>subject</term> <note>original: "subiecti"; the physical matter or substrate in which the essence is housed.</note>, and only so long as it remains within it. Once set free, however, it returns to its own unity. This is indeed one of the more hidden secrets of nature, through which the philosophers reached higher things. You shall therefore separate from any body its spirit along with the soul, as follows:

->### BEYOND NATURE<-

...[secret] which we shall now also show by examples, through which you may be able to learn better than by mere rules—especially since we have made mention of <term>philosophical wine</term> <note>original: "vini philosophici"; in an alchemical context, this is not ordinary wine, but a highly refined spirit or "living" solvent used to extract the virtues of other substances.</note>.

Take grains of wheat, barley, or rye; having first soaked them in pure water so that they swell, let them be taken out and piled into a heap upon the floor, so that after several nights and days they may grow warm and <term>germinate</term> <note>original: "germinet"; this describes the malting process where the grain begins to sprout, "awakening" its internal life or enzymes.</note>. Once this is observed, let them be spread out so that they can be dried. Or, if necessity urges, let them be spread in a wide basket woven of wicker, placed over a furnace that resembles an inverted chimney—that is, narrow at the bottom and wide at the top—and let them be dried by the smoke raised from below, until they appear half-toasted but not burnt, yet showing a reddish color.

Meanwhile, care must be taken that the basket does not catch fire, but is raised high enough away from the flame.

Let this grain be ground in a mill, though not too finely; or let it be broken in a coarse manner—not into flour, but into small fragments. Afterward, let it be placed in a wooden vessel, and let very hot water that has boiled for a long time be poured over it, and let the vessel be covered very well. 

After the liquid has cooled and settled, let it be drawn off through a hair-sieve or a bag, so that the fragments do not mix with it. When you have separated this first water, pour on more boiling water as before and strain it. Repeat this several times until you see that all the <term>substance</term> <note>original: "substantiam"; the essential nutrient or sugary extract (wort) from the grain.</note> has passed from the grain into the waters; which you will easily perceive by the taste of both the liquid and the grain.

Finally, take all the liquids and, in an iron [cauldron]... <note>The text breaks off here; "cacabo fer-" likely refers to "cacabo ferreo," an iron pot or cauldron.</note>

This can be resolved on a marble stone in a cool, damp place into tartaric water, and so on.

### *On the ornament of the metaphysical heaven.*
#### CHAPTER IV.

The lower stars for us are any individuals produced by nature in this lower world through the conjunction of the stars and the upper heaven with the lower elements. Even now, I hear the voice of those growling against us, saying: "Fie! Away with such men, who presume to be able to join heaven to earth." Let them shout louder until they are hoarse; they shall be forced to hear even greater things. Heaven is celestial matter and a universal form, containing within itself all distinct forms, which nonetheless proceed from a single universal form. Therefore, whoever knows how individuals can be reduced through the <term>metaphysical art</term> <note>Here, "metaphysics" refers to the alchemical practice of reducing a physical object to its essential, non-material "form" or essence.</note> into the <term>most general genus</term> <note>original: "genus generalissimum"; the highest possible category of classification, representing the pure essence of a substance before it becomes a specific thing.</note>, and afterwards how to fix special or multiple virtues into it, will easily find the <term>universal medicine</term> <note>original: "medicina vniuersalis"; also known as the Panacea, a substance believed to cure all diseases and prolong life by restoring the body's internal balance.</note>, which heals corruptions and diseases either particularly or universally.

### *On the definitions and some divisions of the terms which we use in describing this little work. CHAPTER V.*

The greater <term>Metaphysical Work</term> <note>A reference to the "Magnum Opus" or Great Work of alchemy, here framed as a metaphysical or spiritual endeavor as much as a physical one.</note> takes its beginning from its own **chaos**. Therefore, a definition of this and of the other intermediates between the two extremes is necessary. In this context, **Chaos** is properly called the confusion of **particular forms** and their **matters** with their **universal form** and its matter. This is considered in various ways: First, as the confusion of the intellect of men who do not understand this art. Second, as the confusion of the parts constituting this first extreme <note>The "first extreme" refers to the raw, unrefined starting material of the alchemical process.</note>. Third, as the confusion in which the intermediates stand confusedly with their extremes, and successively, each intermediate one to the other—namely, in **potentiality**, nor

From this, you can gain an excellent knowledge of our **Chaos**: because one and the same substance, though by different rationales, behaves passively toward one thing and actively toward the same, and then vice versa. Very few until now have had knowledge of **metaphysical matter**, from which the **metaphysical heaven** arises. For one must take into account the simple and the composite reduced to simplicity through alteration, mixing, and so on.

**Form of things** is the **generative virtue** <note>original: "virtus generatiua"; the innate power of a substance to reproduce itself or transform.</note>, by which each thing can produce its own likeness: just as the form of a man lies hidden in a man, and not in another; of a tree in a tree; and likewise of a metal in a metal. The raw matter must not enter the work, but must be evacuated through **sublimation** <note>A process where a solid is heated into a vapor and then condensed back into a solid, used here to purify the substance from "superfluous earth."</note> to the sides of the sublimatory vessel, so that it may be free from its empty and superfluous earth. This occurs while maintaining the distinction of parts, through nourishments and the changing of one medium into another, and of any quantity and form into another. In the third way, they exist naturally within forms and are joined with heaven. Fourth, truly, as they are separated from form and heaven through the means of alteration, mixture, dissolution, and generation, by the mastery of separating their qualities; from these parts, only the airy part is taken and made to flourish through the craft of rotation, and thus it is mixed with the forms in heaven. It should also be noted that the elements can be drawn out from forms through heaven before the conjunction, so that their **primary matter** or "heaven" may be held to approach more closely to the "most general genus" <note>A term from Aristotelian logic referring to the most fundamental category of being.</note>, and so on.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "marriage" between alchemical principles, transitioning from the conceptual union to the physical "embryo" of the Great Work. It explains "Alteration" as a transformation of forms and "Mixture" as the final synthesis of elements.</meta>

### ARTIFICE

...of the male with the female, the metaphysical [union], and of the latter conceiving from the former that which it ought to conceive. Let this be the beginning of the particular **generation**, by which the effect of our **metaphysical marriage** is perceived by the senses, and the union of the twofold seed into the embryo of the female.

**Alteration** is the conversion of one thing into another through likeness: which is considered in two ways, according to a twofold operation, insofar as it happens by itself. In the first way, it is considered as **forms** <note>original: "formae." In Aristotelian and alchemical terms, "form" is the essential nature or "soul" of a substance that gives it its specific properties.</note> are altered into **heaven** <note>original: "coelum." In this context, "heaven" refers to the purest, most volatile essence of a substance, often called the Fifth Essence or Quintessence.</note> and heaven into forms. In the second way, indeed, let it be understood that forms and heaven are so simply converted that they can be converted again into a "third number," namely into the **perfect medicine** and beyond <note>The "perfect medicine" refers to the Philosopher’s Stone, which was believed to "heal" base metals into gold or provide long life.</note>.

These conversions or alterations are called "simple." There are others which happen by "accident" <note>In the philosophy of the time, an "accident" is a non-essential quality, such as color or temperature, rather than the core nature of the thing.</note>, as when from imperfect things, through the **projection** <term>projection: the final stage of the alchemical process where a small amount of the Stone or "Medicine" is cast upon a base metal to transmute it into gold or silver</term> of the perfect medicine, they become perfect by alteration or transmutation. 

**Mixture** is made from two colors, such as pale and azure, so that from them it may be known that forms and heaven are mixed in the **Great Work** <note>original: "opere maiore." The sequence of stages required to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> without any other addition, just as from the beginning of the generation of a human, nothing else is mixed with the man and woman or their seeds: and in this place, the marriage between forms and heaven occurs. Another mixture also occurs, by which indeed the air and fire of any of these, and the forms of heaven—of alteration, mixture, dissolution, generation, and the matter of heaven—are mixed together with the forms, and so forth.

-># THE SUPERNATURAL<-

**Digestion** <note>original: "Digestio." In alchemy, this refers to a process of slow, constant heat, similar to how an egg is incubated or food is processed in the stomach, allowing substances to mature and refine over time.</note> is defined as the nourishing of the conceived seed by the seed itself. Through this process, a **perfect medicine** is born—and even one that is more than perfect. It is necessary for the **forms** <note>In Aristotelian and alchemical terms, "form" is the essential nature or "soul" of a substance that gives it its specific properties.</note> to be digested within the **heaven** <note>original: "coelum." As established previously, this is the purified, volatile essence or "spirit" of the substance within the vessel.</note> for a long enough duration until the "earlier medicine" (which we discussed above) is achieved. Afterward, whenever the alchemist <note>original: "artifex," literally the artisan or craftsman of the Great Work.</note> sees fit, he can proceed further until reaching the second, more perfect medicine.

From this, dear reader, you may conclude that there is a twofold digestion: one that transitions from the forms and the heaven into the perfect medicine, and another that passes from that perfect medicine and the heaven into an even more perfect state. For this reason, other intermediate steps are necessary, which are quite similar to those mentioned before: namely **mixture**, **alteration**, **dissolution**, **generation**, and **operation**; the only difference is that the results of the earlier stages are somewhat more imperfect than those of the later ones. As soon as a **saffron color** <note>original: "croceus color." Yellow or gold hues often signaled the approaching maturity of the alchemical Stone.</note> appears during digestion, it signifies the forms; whereas **azure** <note>original: "azurinus." A blue or sky-colored tint.</note> signifies our heaven. Any colors appearing between these represent the middle state of the **conjunction** <note>The "marriage" or union of opposites.</note> between the heaven and the form, linking the former to the perfect medicine, or the latter to the most perfect.

**Generation** is considered in various ways in this context (though to provide its true definition: it is the manifestation of hidden forms). First, it is viewed as a stage that generates the **perfect medicine** from the heaven and the forms. Second, it generates the **most perfect medicine** from the perfect one and the heaven. In another sense, it occurs when the process begins with **dissolution** and passes through **alteration** and **digestion** toward the perfect medicine, and within it...

This stage is promoted by nature rather than by artifice, although it is nonetheless stirred into action by artifice within the lesser perfection; in that earlier stage, the skill of the alchemist was more beneficial than nature's own power.

In this later mastery, generation occurs no differently than a fetus in the womb or a bird in the egg. For this reason, it is also called the **philosophers' egg** <note>original: "ovum philosophorum." This term specifically refers to large, singular pearls of extraordinary quality.</note>. These are created in the womb of our **philosophical heaven** <note>original: "coelum philosophicum." As noted previously, this is the purified, volatile spirit or "quintessence" that acts as the medium for the work.</note> through the process of generation, possessing the same natural virtues they would have received from nature itself.

From this, it follows that this heaven is able to attract all the virtues existing within stones, by the power of its own unity or simplicity—or, to speak more accurately, by a **magnetic virtue** <note>original: "virtus magnetica." Not just physical magnetism, but an "occult" force of attraction that allows the spirit to draw properties from physical matter.</note>. In this way, the distinguished **artisan** <note>original: "artifex."</note> can elicit heavenly virtues from natural bodies and enclose them within artificial virtues through the operations of this art. For whatever is placed within this heaven is dissolved; and if the appropriate things are added to the dissolved substance, that which is desired will be achieved: making one thing out of many. From this work, many insights can be gathered pertaining to the creation and artifice of precious stones, through the mixture of matter and form within the heaven by means of the waters, etc.

Soon after this color has arisen, it is seen to be collected in a round form, not unlike the form of the **Moon**, or enclosed by a circular line. The final *rubedo* stage, indicating the completion of the "Red Work" to create gold.</note> arises, by which we are made more certain that the members or parts of our **stone** are gathered together in the third number and perfection.

In alchemy, this refers to the Stone's ability to "grow" or multiply its power and to ferment other metals.</note>. # THE ARTIFICE

Our [substance], from the beginning of its conception, has first been led to the threshold of the appearance of the **red color** <note>original: "rubei coloris." This is the *rubedo*, the final stage of the Great Work, signifying the highest perfection and the birth of the Stone.</note>, and without intermission, a double measure of time must be applied to it, but somewhat more vigorously. When this is completed, that which was sought is acquired—namely, a **hard stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone.</note> wrapped in many superfluities <note>Excess matter or impurities that must be cleaned away from the "newborn" stone.</note>, appearing not unlike a fetus after a woman has given birth. The intermediate stages between the **saffron** and **red** colors consist of the digestion, generation, and mixing perceived through the water, the matter, the form, and the heaven. Through the mixing of these, near their conclusion, the intermediate water, matter, and form appear, though without the [actual] form and heaven. For if the form and heaven were still present in that place, certain parts would by no means be willing to be bound with the others, nor be joined together in the **third number** <note>Likely referring to the triad of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury, or the completion of a specific numerical cycle in the Work.</note>.

The **separation of virtues** <note>The process of isolating the essential spiritual or energetic qualities of the substance.</note> is an extraction performed by the motion of **fire** in this particular **mastery** <note>original: "magisterio." A term for the technical process or "The Great Work" of alchemy.</note>. By this heat, the form, the heaven, and the elements are moved incidentally and are excited through alteration, mixing, dissolution, generation, colors, digestion, separation, and operation toward a lesser or greater perfection. Because the degrees and the intermediate stages, by separating themselves from some things, enter into others; and through this operation of separation, the formal and material virtue is drawn from the forms and heaven toward the **lesser perfection** <note>The "White Work," or the stage of Silver.</note>, and from that toward the **greater** <note>The "Red Work," or the stage of Gold.</note>. And in this way, the forms and the heaven—or that which is less perfect—change their simple forms into a state of greater or lesser perfection; the less perfect itself being of a different form than the forms and heaven are in themselves,

# SUPERNATURAL

And since the greater perfection exists under a different form than the less perfect elements did, the form is the heaven, or the forms and the heaven in their own right. These perfections, while their own proper virtues exist under a third form, retain their power within the transforming **poison** <note>original: "veneno." In alchemy, "poison" or "venom" often refers to the potent, corrosive essence of the base matter that must be "mortified" or transformed to create the medicine of the Stone.</note>. If such an origin did not occur through the aforementioned separation, then neither alteration, nor generation, nor progression would follow, and the work of nature in the **philosopher's stone** <term>Philosopher's Stone: the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life</term> would be destroyed.

This middle stage is an indication of the simple elements existing in "act" within our **heaven** <note>original: "coelo." In this context, "heaven" refers to the refined, spiritualized state of the matter, or the upper part of the glass vessel where vapors condense.</note>, along with the natural elements existing in their forms. It shows that fire, air, water, and earth arise and are also separated from the "heaven" to be mixed with the other things already mentioned. All of these stages are designated by specific **colors** <note>The sequence of colors (usually black, white, yellow, red) guided the alchemist through the stages of the process.</note>.

**Potentiality** here is the same as alteration. By this degree, it is understood that all other degrees and any intermediates exist in potentiality within our **Chaos** <term>Chaos: the "prima materia" or raw, unorganized starting material of the Great Work</term> first; and secondly, they exist in the elements that are potentially within our matter. Therefore, this potentiality is nothing other than the "act" of our heaven, which we discuss more extensively in the **stone-making work** <note>original: "opere lapidifico." The specific technical process of constructing the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. Many people know how to prepare stones, but they do not know how to apply natural virtues to them because they are ignorant of our "heaven." It is in this heaven—and from it, through it, and with it—that the completion of the stones and the increase of their perfection consists.

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of the alchemical "Artifice." # ARTIFICE

<note>original: "Artificium." In alchemical texts, this refers to the practical application of the Great Work—the skillful manipulation of matter through the "Art."</note>

...that forms have their own action and operation, either within the less perfect state or as a completed form. The second principle is that external **fire** <note>The physical heat of the furnace, as opposed to the internal "innate" fire of the substance.</note> has an action upon the **heaven**, the forms, and the other elements, as well as the natural and actual intermediaries, moving them toward a state of lesser or greater perfection. The third principle is that the forms, or the less perfect substance, possess their own action upon the **heaven** <note>original: "cœlum." This refers to the most refined, ethereal part of the matter, often associated with the quintessence.</note>, informing it so that it may arrive at a lesser or greater degree of perfection. The fourth **color** signifies the actions of the operations which the **artisan** <term>Artisan (Artifex): The alchemist who performs the work, acting as a "minister of nature."</term> performs upon all those things mentioned above. He uses the administration of fire as a cause for the **separation** of the heaven and the operation of the forms, and so on. This color also signifies the actions of the perfected substances—both the lesser and the greater—upon extrinsic substances through **projection** <term>Projection: the final stage of alchemy where the Stone is cast upon base metals to transform them into silver or gold.</term>, according to their power to reach either the "red" or the "white" state by which they have been informed. This middle stage denotes the rite and manner of fabrication performed by fire, and by the mixing of the matter with the forms and the **philosophical heaven**, along with the instruments suitable to the art.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The "less perfect" substance is called the **white stone**, which is bounded by only three colors, as is evident to the eye; for it is composed of them. These colors, when brought together into one, denote the perfection of the completed work—namely, the perfection of the heaven, the elements, the alteration, the mixture, the dissolution, the generation, the color, the **digestion** <term>Digestion: a process of slow, gentle heating intended to mature the substance, similar to biological digestion.</term>, the separation, the operation, and the fire. By this means it can be known that the substance [obtains] its forms from the heaven...

->### SUPERNATURAL<-

...and having progressed to the highest point by the power of gradual operations. Whoever arrives at this goal will surpass all the wise of the world in wisdom and true doctrine, and all physicians ignorant of this medical art. For it removes not only the infirmities and the **leprosy of metals** <note>original: "lepram metallorum." In alchemy, "leprosy" refers to the impurities and corrosion found in base metals like lead or copper, which the Philosopher's Stone "cures" by turning them into gold.</note>, but also the so-called incurable diseases of human bodies.

**Fermentation** <term>Fermentation: Not the culinary process, but the stage where the "Stone" is joined with gold or silver to act like a leavening agent, allowing its power to be multiplied.</term> is properly called in this context the multiplication of a lesser perfection and its advancement to a greater perfection. This is achieved through the means of mixing, dissolution, alteration, generation, colors, and digestion. It is done by composing the **heaven** <note>original: "cœlum formam." This refers to the quintessence or the most refined, formal part of the matter.</note> form and the less perfect substance into a mixture, and administering the governance of fire until it passes through all the aforementioned stages. This process leads the generation and the colors toward a greater perfection—far more powerful in quantity, quality, and virtue—moving toward the **red and the white** <note>original: "ad rubeum & album." These refer to the final two stages of the Great Work: the White Stone (Albedo) which makes silver, and the Red Stone (Rubedo) which makes gold.</note>, and toward recovering the health of the human body.

From these things, it is easily gathered that the less perfect passes into the greater by the aforementioned means and the actions of the forms, the heaven, the elements, fermentation, separation, operation, and fire, through due administration. Also, the **artisan** <term>Artisan (Artifex): The alchemist who facilitates the natural process.</term> (whenever he wishes) ought to compose the less perfect substance only once, since it can be multiplied infinitely and brought to a greater state. Afterwards, it can be moved from that state into another, infinitely, just as the human race continues its species through a single generation...

# THE ARTIFICE

Something not unlike this can be said of bread dough: for it produces another [batch] through successive **fermentations** <term>Fermentation: In alchemy, this is the process where a small amount of the Stone is added to a substance to "leaven" it, multiplying its power and transforming the whole mass.</term>. In this place, however, it must be noted that a lesser perfection can progress into a greater and infinite one, by means of the **form and the heaven** <note>original: "forma cœloq;" In alchemical theory, the "heaven" refers to the highest, most refined quintessence or spiritual influence within the vessel that gives the matter its new "form."</note> acting as fermenting agents; this has hitherto been known to very few, even among excellent artisans.

The **Greater Perfection** <term>Greater Perfection: The ultimate state of the Philosopher's Stone when it has reached its highest potency for transmutation and healing.</term> is the final completion of the greater stone, which consists of and is composed of four colors. Two of these colors indicate that the **four elements** <note>The four elements: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water. The goal was to balance these opposing forces into a single, harmonious "Fifth Element" or Quintessence.</note> are present within it with every perfection of virtue, and that the many have been brought together into unity, as is clearly seen in the colors **green and red** <note>original: "viridi rubeoque." While "Red" is the famous final stage (Rubedo), "Green" often refers to the "Green Lion," the raw, acidic energy of the beginning of the work which is here perfected.</note>.

The other colors, however, signify that the heaven and the form exist there with all perfection and the simplicity of unity, through the mastery of the four elements and the aforementioned means, etc.

From this final stage, it can be understood that the Greater Perfection has the power to multiply its own species in a twofold way, and to generate something like itself—namely, in its inner virtue through the aforementioned operations, and in the **transmutation** <term>Transmutation: The core alchemical feat of changing the fundamental nature of a substance, such as turning lead into gold.</term> of external substances. Indeed, it is far more perfect than the Lesser [Perfection] in its virtues and operations, both in the transformation of metals and in the cure of illnesses considered hopeless by all; for this reason, it is not undeservedly called the "Greater Perfection."

# THE ARTIFICE

...it receives <note>Continuing from the previous page: referring to the "universal form" of the Lesser Work</note> from the heaven, the elements, alteration, mixture, dissolution, generation, colors, digestion, fermentation, separation, and operation.

**Matter** in this work is considered in various ways: First, it is taken universally; second, as the **philosophical key** <term>Philosophical Key: A specialized solvent or agent—often secret—used to "open" or dissolve the physical body of a metal to reveal its inner spirit.</term>; third, as oil; fourth, as **sulfur** <note>original: "sulphure." In alchemy, sulfur represents the active, masculine, and combustible principle of matter.</note> to be shaped by the shaping oil—specifically during **inceration** <term>Inceration: The process of adding liquid to a dry substance and heating it gently until it reaches a soft, wax-like consistency (from the Latin 'cera' for wax).</term>, which is the reason this stone arises.

**Body** is understood here as the bodies of imperfect metals, from which the sulfur of nature arises when this philosophical key is operating; and through the form of the shaping oil, the work is prepared by intermediate operations, such as solution and inceration, until the stone which is sought is made.

The **philosophical key** in this context is considered no differently than the **heaven** <note>original: "cœlum." The "heaven" of the work refers to the volatile, purified spirit that rises to the top of the vessel.</note> itself in the Great Work. Through this key must be performed **calcination** <term>Calcination: The process of reducing a solid to a fine powder or ash through the application of intense heat.</term>, dissolution, evacuation, multiplication, the spirit, **exuberant quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum vivum exuberatum." This refers to mercury that has been highly purified and "energized" for the work.</note>, the sulfur of nature, the tincture, the oil, and inceration, by means of sublimation and fire, until the aforementioned sulfur is brought to the completed state of the stone.

**Calcination** is considered in various modes. First, when it is performed by **common Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium vulgi." Ordinary quicksilver, which alchemists distinguished from their own "Philosophical Mercury."</note>, under which **Sun** and **Moon** <note>In alchemical code, the Sun (*Sol*) represents gold and the Moon (*Luna*) represents silver.</note> suffer. Second, when it is done through the strongest vinegar, by which **saffron** <note>original: "crocus." In this context, it refers to a reddish-orange metallic oxide, likely *Crocus Martis* (iron oxide).</note> is drawn out from **Mars** <note>Mars is the alchemical symbol for iron.</note>. Third, when by means of sulfur and **orpiment** <note>original: "auripigmentoque." Orpiment is a deep orange-yellow arsenic sulfide mineral.</note> as intermediaries, **burnt copper** <note>original: "æs vstum." Copper oxide produced by heating copper with sulfur or salts.</note> is made from **Venus** <note>Venus is the alchemical symbol for copper.</note>. Fourth, indeed, when—

<meta>This page continues the technical definitions of alchemical processes. The previous page ended while describing calcination; this page explains that calcination is achieved via the "philosophical key" and proceeds to define Dissolution, Evacuation, Multiplication, Spirit, and Exuberated Quicksilver.</meta>

->SUPERNATURAL<-

[Calcination] <note>Continuing from the previous page's definition of calcination.</note> is done by the **philosophical key**, and in this way all **bodies of metals** are prepared to enter into solution, evacuation, and the other operations mentioned before.

**Dissolution** of the manifest previous form of the said bodies is a hiding <note>original: "occultatio." In alchemy, "hiding" a form means breaking down its physical appearance to reach its inner essence.</note>, not by itself but by means of the said key, which is very harmonious with the bodies: just as we see water being transformed by fire by means of the air, without which it is not raised into vapors. It differs from the "greater dissolution" in that by this process only the forms and the less perfect parts are resolved, whereas the greater dissolution resolves any bodies of metals whatsoever.

**Evacuation** is nothing other than the extraction of hidden spirits from their bodies, so that the other forms of the shaping oils may be formed, and in this way, after fermentation and **inceration** <term>Inceration: A process of softening a substance to a wax-like consistency.</term>, they may pass into the stone. That which is indeed drawn from the aforesaid bodies by this operation is the matter from which our stone must be led from potentiality into actuality, through the means and operations already described.

**Multiplication** here is nothing other than the repetition and continuation of the evacuations. Through this operation, the **six bodies** <note>The "six bodies" typically refer to the base metals: lead, tin, iron, copper, mercury, and silver.</note> are provoked to nausea, and to vomiting up the stone from their internal organs <note>original: "visceribus." Alchemists often used biological metaphors—like nausea and vomiting—to describe the violent process of extracting a pure substance from a raw ore.</note>.

**Spirit** is the **quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum vivum," literally "living silver" or mercury.</note> drawn from the six bodies and existing in the waters of the evacuations.

**Exuberated quicksilver** is the spirit of the bodies, already separated through completed evacuation.

# THE CRAFT

**Sulphur** is the spirit of nature within bodies, exalted through **sublimation** into a most excellent salt of four colors: namely, pale, green, black, and red. This last color denotes fire, and through it, a transition is made to the "sulphur of nature," which arises from **exuberant quicksilver** <note>original: "argento vivo exuberato." In alchemy, "exuberant" mercury refers to quicksilver that has been purified or "animated" so that it is ready to combine with metals.</note>. This indicates that the sulphur derives its origin from the spirit, and the spirit from the **multiplication** which proceeds from **evacuation**; and evacuation from **dissolution**, and dissolution from **calcination**, which is performed upon all bodies. 

Furthermore, this sulphur is a "son" born from the imperfect <note>The "imperfect" refers to base metals like lead or tin, as opposed to the "perfect" metals, gold and silver.</note>, and is prepared to put on **white or purple garments** <note>These "garments" refer to the Albedo (whitening) and Rubedo (reddening) stages of the Great Work.</note>. The "son" who is generated from the perfect metals differs from this one by the oil in which the forms of the imperfect sons are contained; and because their sulphur becomes that of nature, as well as the sulphur of others whose sulphurs are dry and susceptible to moisture.

**Tinctures** are the colors appearing in the work in various ways. First, green and saffron. Second, red and saffron, green and black.

The first colors denote the tincture itself, with which the sulphur of nature must be stained; it derives its origin from the **Sun** <note>Gold</note> and **Moon** <note>Silver</note> through the artifice of the operations mentioned above. The other colors signify that the sulphur of any imperfect bodies whatsoever must put on these colors, so that from them the **Stone** may be made, possessing the power of its parents from whom it had its origin. If these two sulphurs have not been joined together like brothers: who, by the said <margin>Sulphur, Quicksilver, Calcination, Tinctures, Sun, Moon, Stone</margin>

# SUPERNATURAL

...be clothed in colors, that which is sought will not be achieved; nevertheless, three or more sulphurs can be joined together at the same time.

**Oil** is the form possessing a double color, namely green and saffron, by which it is indicated that it is produced from the **perfect bodies** <note>Gold and silver</note> through appropriate intermediates.

**Inceration**, **fermentation**, or rather **shaping** <note>original: "informatio."</note> is truly called the formation of the offspring of the imperfect <note>The "offspring" of base metals like lead or tin</note> so that they may receive the condition of the Stone.

**Sublimation** is defined as refinement <note>original: "subtiliatio."</note>, which occurs in two ways: namely, into a most subtle powder, and into a liquid. By this latter path, through the means of multiplication, the spirits of the bodies are converted into **exuberant quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum vivum exuberatum." In alchemy, this is mercury that has been purified and "animated" so it is ready to be joined with metals.</note>; by the former path, however, they are turned into the **sulphur of nature**. Sublimation is also considered in another sense: insofar as the matter becomes closer to perfection—just as exuberant quicksilver is closer than the spirit, and as the sulphur of nature exists closer to the **Stone** than that mercury.

The **Stone** is the end goal and the perfection of all imperfect bodies and operations; within it, they rest as if in fire, like **salamanders** <note>The salamander was a mythical creature believed to live in fire; here it symbolizes a substance that has become "fixed" and can endure the heat of the furnace without being consumed.</note>. This is a "third thing" consisting of matter and form, existing above nature and yet through the medium of nature, forged by the industry of the craftsman.

The **Elements** are considered in two ways in this context: first, as the fire acting from the outside; second, as an external agent—such as that which is drawn from the perfect bodies into the oil, and so forth.

<meta>This page continues the technical definitions of alchemical theory, moving from specific substance definitions (oil, stone) to the cyclical process of nature that the alchemist must emulate—specifically the "wheels" of corruption and generation.</meta>

# THE ARTIFICE

### *Concerning other distinctions of the terms previously defined, and the divisions necessary to be known. CHAPTER VI.*

Nature, when corrupting any individual thing <note>A specific, physical entity</note>, first causes it to rot <note>original: "putrefacit."</note>; afterward, she separates the composite elements that are most closely joined within it. Subsequently, she leads each of these elements toward their highest simplicity, and finally to their **prime matter** <note>The formless "first material" from which all things are made</note>. Once this is finished, the **wheel of corruption** is complete, and from that prime matter, the **wheel of generation** takes its beginning, passing back toward the simple elements. Finally, through the mixing of these, composite elements arise, from which an "elementated subject" <note>A physical body composed of the four elements</note> is made, according to its own specific type and form, under which the elements are drawn together into the composition of an individual thing.

The **metaphysical craftsman** <note>The alchemist who works not just with chemicals, but with the underlying laws of reality</note> must observe this process, so that where nature has finished, he himself may begin by the logic of corruption in a "backward step"—that is, starting from the end and moving through the middle toward the beginning of the wheel of corruption. To do this, he employs various means, which are **calcination** <note>Reduction to powder by heat</note>, **dissolution**, **putrefaction** <note>Rotting or decomposition</note>, **division**, **purification**, and so on.

Thereafter, one must come to the **wheel of generation**, the means of which are **mixing**, **inceration** <note>Softening a substance to a wax-like consistency</note>, **decoction** <note>Concentrating a substance through boiling</note>, and so on. When these are perfectly arranged, they are the instruments of the **Stone**; and when all these things have been rightly performed, the craftsman shall possess what he desired.

The **Form** of this work is considered in a triple state: First, insofar as it is the **first cause**. Second, as the **craftsman**. Third, as any of the other **intermediates** <note>The stages or substances between the start and the end of the work</note>.

<meta>catchword: Matter</meta>

<vocab>Nature, corruption, generation, elements, prime matter, wheel of corruption, wheel of generation, metaphysical craftsman, calcination, dissolution, putrefaction, division, purification, mixing, inceration, decoction, the stone</vocab>

<summary>This page explains the alchemical theory of the "Two Wheels"—Corruption and Generation—arguing that the alchemist must reverse nature's process of decay to reach prime matter before rebuilding it into the Philosopher's Stone. It outlines the specific chemical stages, such as calcination and decoction, required to navigate these cycles.</summary>

<keywords>wheel of corruption, wheel of generation, metaphysical craftsman, prime matter, calcination, inceration, alchemical process, transmutation, decomposition</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical definitions of alchemical theory, moving from the philosophical concepts of the "wheels" of nature to the specific ways in which the alchemist (or "Metaphysical Craftsman") must understand the components of the Great Work, such as matter, heaven, and the elements.</meta>

# SUPERNATURAL
<note>original: "SVPER NATVRALE." This likely refers to the "Supernatural Art" or the alchemical process that works above the standard speed of nature.</note>

**Matter** is considered in just as many ways. First, as the **subject** <note>The raw starting material or "materia prima"</note> from which our **Mercury** <note>The "Mercury of the Philosophers," a refined spirit, not common quicksilver</note> is drawn. Second, as the elements. Third, as the **sulfur of nature** <note>The active, masculine, and fiery principle within matter that drives growth and form</note>.

**Heaven** <note>original: "Cœlum." In alchemy, this often refers to the "sky" or upper part of the glass vessel, or the "Quintessence" that descends like rain</note> also has a triple consideration here. First, as the "greater heaven" <note>The macrocosm or the celestial spheres</note>. Second, as **vegetable Mercury** <note>A solvent or spirit derived from organic sources, believed to have more "life" than mineral mercury</note>. Third, as the **soul of metals**.

The **elements** are considered in two ways in this place. First, simply <note>In their pure, individual states</note>. Second, by way of composition <note>As they are combined in physical bodies</note>.

**Power** <note>or "Potential"</note> or **alteration** differs in as many ways as there are diverse means <note>The various tools, temperatures, or methods used to change a substance</note>.

**Mixing** has a fourfold meaning. First, the mixing of heaven with form. Second, of air with water. Third, of water with fire. Fourth, of air with water and heaven.

**Dissolution** <note>The process of turning a solid into a liquid spirit</note> has only one single consideration, which is between heaven and forms.

**Generation** has a double meaning. First, the way in which the elements are generated. Second, how the **Stone** <note>The Philosopher’s Stone</note> is generated from these and from heaven.

**Colors** also have a double meaning: the former in the evacuation <note>The purging or "emptying out" of impurities</note> of bodies and the **rectification** <note>The process of purifying a substance through repeated distillation</note> of the elements; the latter, in the generation of the Stone.

The **less perfect** <note>Likely referring to the generation of "imperfect" base metals like lead or copper, as opposed to gold</note> has only one consideration, as is clear from the senses.

**Fermentation** <note>The final stage of the work where the "Stone" is fermented with gold or silver to give it the power to transform other metals</note> has a triple meaning: first from virtue <note>Inherent power or quality</note>, second from quantity, and third from both at once.

->I v<-
<note>Gathering or signature mark for the bookbinder</note>

<summary>This page defines the multiple philosophical and practical meanings of key alchemical terms like Matter, Heaven, and Fermentation, categorizing them based on their role in the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>
