--- Page 216 ---
<meta>This page continues the definitions of alchemical processes, moving from the definition of "Dissolution" started on the previous page into the specific stages of "Separation" and the cyclical "Wheels" of metallic transformation.</meta>

# THE CRAFT
<note>original: "ARTIFICIVM." This refers to the practical application of alchemical theory—the "Artifice" or "Workmanship" of the chemist.</note>

**Separation** also involves a threefold operation: first, between the **qualities of the elements**; second, the **purification and fixation** <note>Fixation is the process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire</note> of those elements; and third, the separation of **vegetable Mercury** <note>A refined spirit or solvent believed to hold life-giving properties</note> and its **dregs** <note>original: "fecum," the sediment or waste matter left after a process</note>.

**Operation** has as many meanings as there are **means** <note>The specific methods, tools, or stages</note> that exist within this art.

The **greater perfection** <note>The final goal of the Great Work, the Philosopher's Stone</note>, like the lesser, has a single operation that is manifest to the senses.

The principles and means of philosophy are present in all the principles and means of this art, just like the **motion of the celestial sphere** <note>The rotation of the heavens</note>, which must be considered as the **form** containing all natural instincts and desires.

The most subtle and powerful means in the work is the **repetition of the wheel of the corruption of metals**, which takes place as follows:

With the **forms** <note>The essential "blueprints" or internal structures of the metals</note> placed into the **heaven** <note>The upper part of the alchemical vessel where vapors gather</note>, buried <note>inhumatis; likely referring to "digestion" in a warm bath of ash or sand</note>, dissolved, and emptied through the practice of the "brief art" until one arrives at the **sulfur of nature** <note>The active, fiery principle within metals</note> within the metals, then the **wheel of corruption** <note>The phase where the old form of the metal is destroyed to release its essence</note> is finished.

**Repetition** <note>original: "Reiteratio," the cyclical repeating of a process to increase potency</note> occurs when the **sublimated earth** <note>The solid matter that has been purified and raised by heat</note> is placed into the heaven, and is dissolved there through **putrefaction** <note>The breakdown of matter, usually marked by a change in color</note> and division. After the **air, fire, and water** have been emptied, the craftsman should once again reduce the earth to its purest **sublimation**. When this is finished, the **wheel of generation** follows with its own principles and means, until it is led to its conclusion. This operation is more subtle than others; though it is longer, it is nonetheless more perfect.

By this path, the "less perfect"—which is a stone not yet **fer-** <meta>catchword: mentated,</meta>

...fermented, it is converted into more, and into the **fermented stone**, through the separation and composition of the elements, which occur as follows. Nature in no way permits the elements to be separated by themselves: for then each would remain alone, possessing only a single quality, and would have no desire for **generation** <note>The alchemical "birth" or creation of new, more perfect substances</note>, which is contrary to nature.

The **separation of the elements** is, in fact, the predominance and victory of one over and upon the other, in a mixed rather than a divided manner. For the color of **fire** is red, and because one must pass through the **sphere of air** <note>In the ancient view of the universe, the elements were arranged in layers or "spheres": Earth at the center, then Water, Air, and finally Fire</note> before reaching the sphere of fire, a **citrine** <note>original: "citrinus"; a lemon-yellow color associated with a specific stage of the alchemical process</note> color appears before the redness—clear and transparent—resulting from the mixture of fire with air. Even before reaching the sphere of air, one must necessarily pass through the **sphere of water**; for this reason, a white and watery color appears brilliantly. But because the **earth** is the center and the starting point from which the ascent must begin, while the stone remains in its sphere, a **black color**—which is proper to the earth—appears within it.

Note, therefore, the separation and joining of these into one, namely **fire**. Furthermore, if a red or white color appears before the black, or any color other than those mentioned above, you must believe that the **operation of the stone** has been ruined, because the necessary sequence of the joining of these states has been broken. One should also note the difference said to exist between the <term>Elixir</term> <note>A medicinal or transformative substance, often liquid</note> and the **stone**, defined by the "raw" and the "cooked." For the **Elixir** is a medicine composed of air, water...

Various errors of the craftsmen must also be noted. The first and most significant consists in this: that they do not know how to **imitate nature** in the slightest. The second is that they mistake the **end of the stone** for its beginning, believing they have finished when they have barely started—as seen in the false opinion regarding the **red triangle** <note>Likely a reference to a specific geometric symbol or a stage in the process that practitioners misidentified</note>.

For the composition of the **Greater Stone** <note>The most potent version of the Philosophers' Stone, capable of transmuting metals and healing</note> lasts two years for those who have never performed the composition before; however, for the experienced, it takes one continuous year and three months. There are some branches of this Art in its vastness that are completed in four or six months. If the craftsman is able to find these, well and good; if not, let him mourn the meager capacity of his own intellect, since they are described clearly enough in this little book. Now, I see that I must address the lesser differences.

**Form** is twofold: namely, **simple** and **composite**. **Simple form** is absolute unity—the singularity of a thing existing in itself and not joined with another. **Composite form** is the union of its four primordial chambers <note>A term for the fundamental structural components or elements of a substance</note>.

**Matter** consists of the **four elements**: either in a simple state, as signified by the first kind of form; or in a composite state, as indicated by the second. By its power, any bodies whatever are dissolved, putrefied, and purified; their elements are separated, and the substance is exalted into a **wonderful salt** <note>original: "sal mirabile"</note> and into earth, by means of the highly attractive power it possesses. Whoever thinks that any other water is intended is ignorant and foolish.

The **soluble menstruum** is a potential vapor existing in any metallic body, joining **sulfur** and **quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum viuum"; mercury</note>, and without it, they are by no means brought into action. Finally, it is defined thus: the Menstruum is the medium joining imperfect and perfect metals into the **Stone**.

This dissolving Menstruum is hot up to the second point of the **fourth degree** <note>The "fourth degree" was the highest level of intensity in the humoral and herbal systems, indicating substances that are extremely caustic, pungent, or toxic</note>. For this reason, its power must be increased with "hot" vegetables up to the final point of that degree; such as **wild celery**, **monkshood**, **oleander**, **cashew nut**, **black pepper**, **pellitory-of-Spain**, **euphorbium**, and so on.

### *On the general practice of the philosopher’s stone, through the wheels of corruption and generation, etc.*
#### CHAPTER VII.

Pour so much of the **heaven** <note>original: "coeli"; referring to the celestial solvent or quintessence of wine described on the previous page</note> over either of the **forms** <note>original: "formarum"; the material substances or "bodies" being treated</note> that it rises four fingers above them. Place this itself in **dung** <note>original: "fimum"; horse manure was used in alchemy as a source of gentle, steady, and natural heat for the process of "digestion"</note> for six natural days <note>A "natural day" refers to a full 24-hour period</note>, or until the form is converted into water. Afterward, remove the vessel and allow the water to evaporate in **ashes** <note>original: "cineribus"; an ash-bath or sand-bath provided a drier, more intense heat than the dung-pit</note>; that is, congeal that which had been dissolved.

Soon after, pour onto this **earth** <note>original: "terram"; the solid residue remaining after evaporation</note> as much of the **heaven** as before, and place it in the dung again for a month and a half, or longer, for the purpose of **putrefying** it <note>Putrefaction was considered a vital stage where the substance "died" and turned black before being reborn in a purified state</note>. Finally, remove the vessel and place it in a **secret furnace**, in which you should allow the water to distill until it no longer drips.

Next, place it in the ashes to be distilled, and a water thicker than the first will emerge, which we call **air**. When this is finished, pour onto the **dregs** <note>original: "fecibus"; the solid matter left behind after distillation</note> a weight of the **heaven** equal to what you used before, and let it sit in the dung for the same amount of time as previously. Then, collect the **air** through the ash-bath until it no longer wishes to drip.

Afterward, let there be a repetition of the pouring of the **heaven** over the earth, and after it has stood for an equal time in the dung, let it be distilled through the ashes again until no more of the **air** comes out. Finally, pour the entire quantity of this **air** onto the dregs remaining in the <term>cucurbit</term> <note>original: "cucurbita"; a gourd-shaped glass vessel used for distilling</note>—or first grind them thoroughly upon a marble stone—and let the **putrefaction** take place in a sealed vessel for forty days within the dung. At last, having removed the vessel and placed it in the ashes, let the **air** and the **fire** be distilled both together; however, beware of...

Again, return what has been distilled to the dregs, continuing this up until the **seventh distillation** <note>The number seven was of great symbolic and practical importance in alchemy, representing a complete cycle of purification</note> with its burials. Afterward, distill through dung: return what has distilled to the dregs; you should do this even up to the seventh distillation, reiterating the pourings, burials, and distillations for their specified days, as has been said. 

Thus far is formed the **triangle** <note>original: "triangulus"; a conceptual or geometric representation of the relationship between the elemental principles in this stage of the work</note> from the air, the heaven, and the dung, in the middle of which is the **soul of the air**. Finally, take the **earth**, and you shall empty it until it is desolate and converted into the **sulfur of nature** <note>original: "sulphur naturæ"; the active, combustible, and masculine principle in alchemy</note>, and a triangle shall be formed from earth, dung, and ashes, in the center of which the **soul of the earth** shall stand. The wheel of corruption is not finished until this point; its instruments are the heaven, dung, the **secret furnace**, and the heat of the ashes, and so forth. For the **compound** <note>original: "compositum"</note> made of fire and air, take four parts of fire for the **sun** <note>original: "solem"; likely referring to gold or the solar principle of the work</note>, and mix it with fifteen parts of air; you shall set this aside for **digestion** <note>prolonged, gentle heating</note> for fifteen days, then afterward **distill** it through dung. You shall repeat this digestion and distillation as many times as necessary until a certain substance remains at the bottom of the vessel like a **shining oil**, forming a single compound of air and fire, which is called **Mud** <term>Limus: literally "silt" or "slime"; a reference to the primordial "slime of the earth" from which life or the Philosopher's Stone is created</term>.

By this same process, you will convert the fire and earth into their own compound, so that a **double quadrangle** is formed: the first from fire, air, dung, and ashes, in the center of which the compound of fire and air shall rejoice. The second is from fire, earth, dung, and ashes as well, in the center of which shall stand the compound of earth and fire. Now take a part of the compound of fire and air, to which you shall mix a sixth part of **heaven**, and put it into digestion for thirty days; thus a triangle...

## THE ARTIFICE

...of the elements themselves, by first waxing them with air and fire, or with the aforementioned filings made from air and fire. In this very artifice, the **wheel of generation** reaches its limit and end, the sign of which for us is the **red triangle** <note>original: "triangulus rubeus"; a symbol for the element of fire and the final completion of the Great Work or Rubedo</note>. The beginning of the work, therefore, is **corruption**, while **generation** is truly the end. Through these operations, one quality is corrupted <note>In alchemy, "corruption" is not rot, but the breaking down of a substance's form so it can be reborn as something else</note> and another is introduced through generation—just as by corrupting water and generating earth. This is done by hiding the earth and manifesting the fire and air, until the **lesser and greater perfection** <term>perfectionem: refers to the two main goals of alchemy—the white elixir (lesser) and the red elixir or Philosopher's Stone (greater)</term> arise through their own media. These media are the heats of **dung**, of **ashes**, and of the **secret furnace** <note>These represent varying intensities of heat: dung provides a gentle, steady warmth; ashes a more moderate heat; and the furnace the highest temperatures</note>, by which water is converted into earth, and this earth into air and fire. 

Therefore, earth is the medium between water, fire, and air; and the dung, ashes, and secret furnace exist as the medium through which fire and air are converted into the **soul of fire and of air** <term>animam: the volatile, spiritual essence of the element extracted during the process</term>. Wherefore fire and air are the medium between the earth itself, the soul of fire, and the soul of air. 

Next, the dung, ashes, the secret furnace, and the **heaven** <note>original: "coelum"; likely referring to the upper part of the distilling vessel where vapors collect</note> are the medium through which the soul of fire and the soul of air are converted into the **compound of fire and air**, and into the **compound of earth and fire**. By this reasoning, the heaven is the medium between the soul of fire, the soul of air, the compound of air and fire, and the compound of fire and earth. Likewise, the dung, ashes, and secret furnace are the medium through which the compound of fire and air, and also the compound of earth and fire, are joined to the elements. In the same way, the compound of fire and air...

### *Concerning the specific practice of the stone from the minor work.* CHAPTER VIII.

Place the **metallic body** into the **philosophical key** <note>original: "clauem philosophicam"; a secret solvent or corrosive agent used to break down metals</note> contained in its own vessel, and allow it to digest for one week in heat of the first degree or of vapor <note>In alchemy, the "first degree" of heat is the gentlest, often compared to the warmth of a brooding hen or a sun-warmed day</note>. But before it is placed into the key, it must be reduced to a **calx** <note>original: "calcem"; a powder produced by heating a metal to remove its "moisture," a process called calcination</note>, so that the body may be better dissolved and evacuated by means of the key and the heat of the first degree from the **triangle**, according to the natural order. 

Once the dissolution of the body is completed, let the vessel be placed for **distillation**, excited by the aforementioned heat; it will lose the key and be reduced into earth. Allow this earth, after being thoroughly ground upon a marble stone, to be resolved into water by itself. But if the earth should be too dry, let it be sprinkled with a little of the key, and after three days it will begin to be resolved into a **composite water** containing the **four elements**. 

Distill this water through a filter and repeat the solution of that earth which remains in the filter, until a good quantity of water seems to be present to you. By this operation, the **triangle of evacuation** <term>triangulus euacuationis: a symbolic or physical stage where the substance is "emptied" of its previous form and impurities</term> shall be completed, in the center of which is the earth, which must "drink up" its own water, as follows: Place the filtered water into heat of the first degree to digest, and it will cast out the key entirely and be converted into small stones—which, indeed, are not yet the **elixir** <term>elixir: the refined medicinal or transformative substance that is the goal of the alchemist's work</term>. By the aforementioned evacuation, another triangle is completed...

ARTIFICE

...from water, by heat of the first degree <note>the gentlest heat, often compared to the warmth of the sun or a nesting bird</note> and the key, in the midst of which the said stones exist. Grind these stones very finely and place their powder into putrefaction <note>original: "putrefactionem"; a stage of breakdown or "death" of the material required before it can be reborn or transformed</note>. In this way, it will be converted into a metallic water or oil, in which the four elements are contained. Divide their qualities, as you have heard. Once these are divided, purify the earth—or return the finest part of it through the required sublimations <note>original: "sublimationibus"; the process of heating a substance into vapor and then solidifying it again to purify it</note>—and finally saturate it with its own elements: first with the heavenly element, then the airy water, and lastly with the fiery element, as is the custom of the art. Let them be boiled down to the perfection of which you have heard. According to this process, the extreme and middle principles of this art are applied to whatever metaphysical work <note>original: "opus metaphysicum"; refers here to the higher spiritual or philosophical goals of alchemy beyond simple chemistry</note> you desire.

### *On the coherence of the metaphysical operations of both practices.* CHAPTER IX.

In a wonderful way, the instruments of these works are connected—namely, the intellectual means with the sensory ones. This is similar to how the **form** (which represents the persona of the craftsman) acts as an existing instrument through which nature generates our particular **egg** <note>original: "ouum"; a common alchemical term for the vessel or the primordial material of the Great Work</note> from the intellect. Although this must be distinguished from our "form" (which we otherwise call "heaven" <note>original: "coelum"; in alchemy, this often refers to the volatile, spiritualizing essence or the highest part of the apparatus</note>), nevertheless the intellectual form cannot persist without the sensory form in our craft, nor vice versa. For our "heavenly form" is the principle and the sensory medium that generates, nourishes, and causes growth or flourishing; it moves the elements toward a specific generation <note>original: "generationem"; the birth or creation of a new substance</note>, just as the greater heaven moves the lower things. And by the forming form, which through the vegetable...

This is also true for many other things hidden within the process of the art, which are possible for the craftsman to know and achieve. 

But the **form** which is the **human intellect** <note>original: "intellectus"; the reasoning and spiritual mind of the alchemist</note> is the beginning, the middle, and the end in the preparations. This is denoted by a **saffron color** <note>original: "croceo colore"; a golden-yellow hue often symbolizing the Sun, Sulfur, or the perfected state of the soul</note>, by which it is indicated that man is the greater and principal **form** in this metaphysical work, more powerful even than the **form** of the heavens. 

Other **forms**, however, are lesser than these, as they exist in states of greater or lesser perfection. The equal mixing of the **saffron** with the **green color** signifies that among all these forms there must necessarily be a harmony—whether they are greater or lesser—without opposition, working toward the equality of the ultimate goal. 

For one of the three **forms** cannot be absent without all of them failing at once. Indeed, if the **heavens** <note>original: "coelo"; referring to the celestial or astral influences and the "heavenly" quality of the materials</note> were failing, the **generation** <note>the process of coming into being or growing</note> of the elements would fail, and a deprivation of both greater and lesser perfection would follow. Therefore, the **intellectual form**, along with the **vegetable form** <note>original: "forma vegetabili"; the principle of life and growth found in plants and minerals</note> and the **form of motion**, must be so joined through the intellect that one is conditioned by the other; and so it goes through the remaining degrees and means until the most perfect state is reached. 

The **form** which is called **motive** <note>original: "motiva"; the principle of movement or change</note> is a certain universal principle in the degrees of this art, moving each stage to the next through all the degrees, even until the end. Thus, **motion** can be called a **form**, stirring all the degrees in order, until...

### *Concerning the Most Excellent Practice of the Philosophers' Stone.* CHAPTER X.

Take one of the forming bodies, mixed with **common mercury** <note>mercurio vulgi; ordinary quicksilver used as a base</note> and **common salt**, and let it be **calcined** <note>reduced to a powder through high heat</note>. Let this **silver** <note>argentum; often referring to the purified metallic seed</note> be **sublimated** from it, or be received as **quicksilver** in water as you know how, and let the salt be diluted with warm water. 

Afterward, divide the qualities of the **elements**, and to three pounds of **our heaven** <note>original: "cœli nostri"; in alchemy, this refers to the purified, volatile spirit or the "mercury of the philosophers" which acts as a celestial solvent</note>, add two ounces of the **lesser form**, and for the **greater form** you shall take two and a half ounces with half a pound of the **heaven**. From these you shall make a **marriage** <note>coniugium; the chemical union or "conjunction" of the male/sulfuric and female/mercurial principles</note>; you shall place them for mixing in a vessel closed extremely well, and you shall nurture them with continuous heat until, through dissolution, the matter passes into **alteration**, and another color begins to arise—namely a black color, not entirely, but in the manner of a dark **pomegranate** <note>original: "mali granati"; describing a deep, dark red-black hue that signals the beginning of the "nigredo" or black stage of the process</note>.

By this sign, you shall judge that the **form** (or the forms) and the **heaven** are passing through alteration, ge- <note>continued on next page as "generation"</note>

When that space of time is finished, he must allow it to be cooled. Now he will find **our infant** in the manner of an egg; having been extracted and cleaned, let him place it in a clean glass vessel, and again into the furnace...

One part of its weight, **projected** <note>original: "proiectum"; <term>Projection</term> is the final step of alchemy, where a small amount of the Stone is cast upon base metal to transform it into gold or silver.</note> upon three thousand parts of matter, can multiply its own form; and by a third repetition, it can be projected upon three hundred thousand parts. The **multiplication** <note><term>Multiplication</term> is the process of increasing the quantity and power of the Alchemical Stone through repeated cycles of dissolution and coagulation.</note> of this, as with the prior "greater perfection," can also be achieved.

Colors are the best indicators of this **artifice** <note>The "Artifice" refers to the skilled practice of the alchemical work.</note>, by which it must also be guided. But when the final color appears—namely, the **red** <note>The "Rubedo" or redness is the final triumph of the Great Work, indicating the creation of the "Red Stone" which transforms metals into gold.</note>—know that the body has been joined with the soul.

Once this is perceived, the fire must be continued, as stated above, until the birth. Just as has been said here regarding the colors of the **red infant**, the same should be understood regarding the colors of the **white infant** <note>The "White Infant" or White Stone is a lesser version of the Stone used to transmute metals into silver rather than gold.</note> according to its own qualities. By these signs, the craftsman will be able to undertake the metaphysical work without fear and complete it successfully.

## Concerning the less arduous practice of the philosopher's stone. CHAPTER XI.

Take the calx <term>Calx</term> <note>A powder produced by heating a metal or mineral to high temperatures until it oxidizes or crumbles.</note> of any body and place it in a long-necked philosophical vessel <note>original: "vas philosophicum"; usually a glass flask such as a bolthead or retort.</note>. Pour over this the juice of the philosophical key <term>Philosophical key</term> <note>original: "clauis"; a metaphor for a specialized solvent or reagent used to "unlock" the properties of the matter.</note> until it stands four fingers deep above the material. Afterward, let it boil gently for a natural day <note>A period of 24 hours.</note> over slow fire on a bed of ashes. Following this, place it in warm dung <note>original: "fimo"; decaying manure was used by early chemists as a source of steady, gentle, and low-level heat for long-term incubation.</note> or, even better, a bath <note>balneóve; a water bath or "bain-marie."</note> so that it may be digested <term>Digestion</term> <note>A process of slow, continuous heating intended to break down substances into more refined parts.</note>, and the material may be separated into its subtle parts—that is, the pure from the impure.

When the designated time is finished, remove the vessel. By tipping it carefully so that the dregs <note>original: "fecibus"; the sediment or solid waste left at the bottom.</note> are not disturbed, pour off the clear water into another vessel. Having covered this vessel, place the collected liquid back into the dung to be digested again. Once this evacuation <note>The process of drawing out the liquid extract.</note> is complete, pour on another "key" similar to the first, so that it overflows as we said before. Make it boil and return it to the dung as before, and so on.

You shall repeat these operations as many times as necessary until the body is emptied of its spirits <note>Alchemists believed that physical matter ("body") contained volatile, transformative essences ("spirits") that had to be extracted.</note>. If you run out of the "key," you shall distill all the waters from the evacuations through an alembic <term>Alembic</term> <note>A classic distillation apparatus with a cooling "cap" that allows vapors to condense and drip into a receiver.</note> over a slow fire—specifically a water bath—until you have two parts of the evacuation remaining.

Pour as much of this water onto the material as was stated above, continuing this process until the earth <note>original: "terra"; the solid residue remaining after the spirit has been extracted.</note> is empty. You shall test this in the following way: take a little of that earth, having first dried it in the sun, and place it upon a glowing-hot plate; if it emits smoke, repeat the operations mentioned until it no longer smokes. Distill afterward all the liquids from these evacuations through an alembic and a Mary's bath <term>Mary's bath</term> <note>original: "Mariae balneum"; a water bath used to provide gentle, even heat, known today as a bain-marie.</note>, until the matter appears at the bottom of the vessel like melted wax or like honey. When the bath has cooled, pour back onto the matter the water which you had drawn off through distillation, so that it stands four fingers deep above it, and expose it to the digestion of dung <note>original: "fimi"; decaying manure was used to provide a steady, low heat for long-term incubation.</note> or a bath for a natural day <note>A 24-hour period.</note>, keeping the vessel closed. Distill afterward through the alembic as you have heard, and when the vessel is cool, return the drawn-off water again to the matter; digest, distill, return, and continue the repetitions of this process until you perceive that the whole matter has ascended together with the water. This matter is called "virgin's milk" <term>Virgin's milk</term> <note>original: "lac virginis"; a traditional alchemical name for a white, cloudy solution or a specific stage of mercurial refinement.</note> by the philosophers, and "exuberated quicksilver" <term>Exuberated quicksilver</term> <note>original: "argentum vivum exuberatum"; mercury that has been processed to increase its perceived spiritual or chemical potency.</note>.

For the salt of the perfect metals <note>original: "perfectorum"; referring to gold and silver, which alchemists considered "perfect" because they do not rust or perish in fire.</note> has a fixing power <note>original: "virtutem... fixatiuum"; the ability to make a substance stable and resistant to evaporation by fire.</note> over all things, having been drawn from the imperfect salts which we mentioned. # THE ARTIFICE

By which you will afterwards extract the qualities of the elements <note>The four Aristotelian elements: Fire, Air, Water, and Earth, which alchemists believed were the building blocks of all matter.</note> as follows. First, by means of a most gentle **bath** fire <note>original: "ignem balnei"; likely a water bath or *balneum Mariae*, which provides a steady, mild heat that does not exceed the boiling point of water.</note>, you should receive as much water as can rise with that heat. When this distillation stops and the vessel has cooled, pour the entire distilled liquor back onto its dregs <note>original: "feces"; the solid residue or "earth" left behind after a liquid has been boiled off.</note>, and place this vessel containing it, well-sealed, into **digestion** <term>Digestion</term> <note>A process of slow, continuous heating, often using warm horse dung or a water bath, used to soften or mature a substance over many days.</note> in dung or a bath.

Afterwards, by means of ashes and a fire built of iron filings <note>original: "ferratura"; likely referring to the iron grate or iron-rich fuel used in a furnace to achieve higher temperatures than a water bath.</note> in a furnace governed by **registers** <term>Registers</term> <note>original: "registris"; sliding plates or dampers used to control the airflow and precisely regulate the temperature of the furnace.</note>, distill all the oil that can rise with that heat. Distill this—distill it again by the heat of a bath: that which rises, some call "water"; that which remains at the bottom, they call "air."

Pour this distilled water back onto the dregs again, and having first placed it in digestion for seven days, draw from it first with a gentle fire until all the water has passed through the **alembic** <term>Alembic</term> <note>The apparatus used for distilling, consisting of a gourd-shaped base and a cap with a downward-pointing tube to collect condensed vapors.</note>. Afterwards, having increased the heat to the **fourth degree** <note>The highest and most intense level of heat in alchemical practice, typically a strong, direct fire.</note>, drive upward whatever liquor there is, until the dregs and an impalpable powder—which should be ground on marble—remain at the bottom of the **gourd** <term>Cucurbit</term> <note>original: "cucurbitæ"; the lower, flask-like vessel of the distilling apparatus.</note>.

However, you will distill the liquor placed in the bath at that heat until nothing more can rise. When the distillation is finished, an oil will remain at the bottom of the vessel, which they call "fire," and another liquor driven by the bath, "water," and so on.

Once this evacuation is performed, to the weight of the aforementioned powder, add half its weight of the aforementioned **quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum viuum"; referring here to the "mercury of the philosophers" or metallic essence described on page 221, rather than common liquid mercury.</note> by means of soaking; place it in a closed vessel in digestion of dung or a bath for eight days. When the time of this digestion is finished—

<meta>catchword: finished (fing-)</meta>

<summary>This page provides a complex, multi-step distillation process intended to separate the "elements" of a substance by varying the intensity of the heat. The final goal is to produce a refined powder to be recombined with the metallic "quicksilver" for further transformation.</summary>

# SUPERNATURAL

Perform each step as described in the other transformations: note this, that every substance possesses its own unique **living silver** <note>original: "argentum vivum"; mercury, considered by alchemists to be the universal metallic spirit or the "seed" of metals.</note>. Once the **sublimation** <term>Sublimation</term> <note>The process of heating a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again on a cooler surface, used to purify the "spirit" of the material.</note> is complete, take the material that has gathered on both the sides and the top of the vessel and place it into a well-sealed **cucurbit** <note>A gourd-shaped glass vessel used in distillation.</note>. Set it to digest in warm dung or a **water bath** <note>original: "Mariae balneum"; literally "Mary's bath," a vessel of water used to provide gentle, indirect heat.</note> for eight days. When these days are finished, if the matter is not yet dissolved, let it remain longer until it is resolved into a clear liquid, which is called the **unguent** and **oil of the philosophers**. If the substance happens to be resistant to dissolving, add a small amount of the already known salt—which has been dissolved and congealed two or three times—which is "our heaven," and add also the **philosophic key** <note>original: "clavis philosophica"; a reagent or solvent used to "unlock" or dissolve metals.</note>, which has been purified four times, and it will dissolve perfectly. Do this same thing in all your dissolutions.

Finally, place the salt of any metal into a **crucible** <note>original: "tigillo"; a ceramic or metal container used for melting materials at high temperatures.</note> heated over ashes. You shall "wax" <note>original: "inceratis"; the process of *inceration*, or adding liquid to a dry substance drop by drop until it reaches a soft, waxy consistency.</note> a portion of it with the aforementioned oils, pouring them in drop by drop until the mixture reaches the thickness of cold honey. Then remove it from the fire and hold a small piece of this mass to a candle flame. If it melts easily, it is well done; if not, repeat the incerations until the matter flows before the **flight of Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurii fugam"; the point at which the volatile mercury or spirit begins to evaporate and escape the heat.</note>. You may also perform this inceration using the **quintessence** <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>The purest and most concentrated form of a substance, considered the fifth element or celestial essence.</note> of the perfect metals, in the manner described above. The aforementioned oils are incombustible.

->THE ARTIFICE<-

# *On the method of extracting the heaven, or the philosophic key, from*
# *vegetables such as new honey, celandine, flowers,*
# *human blood, and also from both types of wine.*
## CHAPTER XII.

Once the qualities of the elements have been drawn from the vegetables by the aforementioned artifice—specifically from those substances listed below—let only their **fire** <note>original: "ignis"; in alchemy, this refers to the most active, essential, and transformative spirit within a substance.</note> be taken. This must be **rectified** <term>Rectification</term> <note>The process of refining a substance, usually through repeated distillation, to achieve extreme purity.</note> in the same manner that was described regarding the **philosophical wine**. By this path, our **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"; here referring to the universal animating spirit rather than common quicksilver.</note> will be prepared with the highest **exaltation** <note>The process of raising a substance to a higher state of dignity or power.</note>.

A mixture can nevertheless be made of the "heaven" of new honey, **celandine** <note>original: "chelidonia"; a plant with yellow sap highly prized by alchemists for its perceived solar properties.</note>, rosemary flowers, **mercury herb** <note>original: "Mercurialis"; the plant *Mercurialis annua*, used in medicinal and alchemical preparations.</note>, red lily, human blood, etc., combined with the "heaven" of red or white wine, or of **tartar** <note>original: "tartari"; the potassium bitartrate crust found in wine barrels, used as a source of "salt."</note>. A mixture can also be made of those things mentioned above, once they have been reduced into "heaven" and taken in equal parts, combined with the "forms" according to the weight declared in this **Little Work** <note>original: "Opusculo"; referring to the present book.</note>. Following the order of the doctrine and the practice of the **Great Work** <note>original: "maioris operis"; the Magnum Opus, the ultimate goal of alchemy to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, this substance can be brought to a "lesser perfect work."

From there, another mixture can be made: namely, of the "heaven" combined with the **philosophic key** <note>original: "claue philosophica"; a specialized solvent or reagent used to "unlock" the internal virtues of a substance.</note>, through the aforementioned artifice of generation. Afterward, let the "lesser perfect" substance mentioned above be added, according to the proportion of the Great Work; in this way, one passes to the "greater perfect," which is able to multiply its own likeness into external substances by **ten thousandfold** <note>original: "bis quinque numero milibus"; literally "twice five in number of thousands." This refers to the power of projection, where one part of the medicine can transform ten thousand parts of base material.</note>, etc.

...heaven, together with the heaven of red or white wine, or of **tartar** <note>original: "tartari"; the mineral crust formed in wine casks, used as a source of salts.</note> drawn through the **mastery** <term>Magisterium</term> <note>The expert execution of a complex alchemical process or the resulting powerful substance.</note> of the separation of qualities from the elements, and placed with the form or for the sake of the form, according to the teaching of the **Greater Work** <note>original: "maioris operis"; the final goal of alchemy, the creation of the Philosopher's Stone or the ultimate medicine.</note>. Once the vessel is sealed again, let it be cooked with continuous heat for another fifteen days.

When the artifice of mixing is complete, you will see the mixture transmuted into a less-than-perfect state through intermediate colors. The craftsman must guide the work according to these colors until it reaches the most perfect Greater Work, which has the power to transmute its own species into external substances and to **multiply** <note>The alchemical process of increasing the potency or quantity of the transmuting agent.</note> its own weight by three thousand times, etc.

### *On the universality of the philosophical Mercury.*
### CHAPTER XIII.

Our **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"; here referring to the universal animating spirit or "Mercury of the Philosophers," not common quicksilver.</note> is contained in every elemental thing. In some, however, it is so deeply hidden that human life would fail before it could be separated from it. In others, it is so near that it is very easy to extract, such as in those things mentioned in the preceding chapter. Some who know the **secret** <note>original: "arcanum"; a hidden mystery or specialized medicinal/alchemical knowledge.</note>...

# THE ARTIFICE

...this, namely that **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"; here referring not just to the metal quicksilver, but to the vital alchemical principle believed to reside within all things.</note> is present in everything, they have attempted to extract it from stones, rye straw, flowers, and wood—but in vain. They reached the end of their lives sooner than they reached their goal. Some began with things closer at hand, such as **celandine** <note>original: "gelidoniam"; a plant in the poppy family often used in alchemical experiments due to its yellow sap.</note>, rosemary flowers, **mercury herb** <note>original: "mercurialem"; likely *Mercurialis annua*, known for its association with the god Mercury.</note>, and white lily. From their juices, through the **mastery** <term>Magisterium</term> <note>The expert execution of a complex alchemical process.</note> of the **water bath** and **dung heat** <note>original: "balnei fimi que"; referring to the *balneum Mariae* (warm water) and the heat of fermenting horse dung, used for gentle, steady incubation.</note>, they tried to extract our Mercury, which in color, weight, quality, and quantity was not unlike **common mercury** <note>original: "mercurio vulgi"; ordinary liquid quicksilver.</note>. Thinking they had found the greatest secret, they **sublimated** <note>The process of heating a substance to a vapor and collecting the resulting solid deposit.</note> it with corrosives and thereby corrupted its nature. 

Thus it happened that they by no means achieved the goal of their expectations, because they were ignorant of the principles, the means, and the ends of this art, by which our **metaphysical work** <note>original: "opus metaphysicum"; the spiritual or higher-order labor that transcends physical chemistry.</note> is completed. But if you take our Mercury converted into such a form, or take common mercury, and reduce it to water without any material additive (which can be done with the **phlegm** <note>original: "phlegmate"; the watery, tasteless distillate produced during separation.</note> of the medium, if you provide its air to it within the vessel) and distill it seven times, you will have the **luster of pearls** <note>original: "lumen perlarum."</note>. For this is their **ethereal** <note>original: "ætherogenea"; belonging to the celestial or spiritual realm of matter.</note> part, which lacks the condition of our **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone.</note>. 

One must take care, therefore, in our **transmutatory art** <term>Ars transmutatoria</term>, regarding such an extraction of Mercury from individual substances. In some substances, our Mercury has a somewhat free action, as in **tartar** <note>The mineral crust formed in wine barrels.</note>. This is evident from the operations of **goldsmiths** <note>original: "aurifabrilibus."</note>: when there is a bit of **Silver** <note>original: "Lunae"; literally "of the Moon," the alchemical name for silver.</note> in **Copper** <note>original: "Venere"; literally "in Venus," the alchemical name for copper.</note>, and this is placed in white wine,

# SUPERNATURAL.
<page-num>227</page-num>

...or into its soul, the power of our mercury will bring the **tincture of the Moon** <note>original: "Lunæ tincturam"; referring to the silver-producing essence or a silver-like white quality.</note> to the surface. By this, the bond of its harmony with the **Moon** <note>Silver</note> is revealed. It is better when you have extinguished ignited **Venus** <note>original: "Venerē"; referring to Copper</note> in **tartar** <note>original: "tartaro"; potassium bitartrate, a byproduct of winemaking used here as a purifying agent.</note>, which has been dissolved by white wine; it will then take on a whiteness. Yet this color is not permanent, because it lacks a fixed spiritual subject, and so forth. Another experiment is also performed with it, where **Mars** <note>Iron</note> has been frequently extinguished in the aforementioned solution. It takes on the softness of the **Sun** <note>Gold</note> and the **Moon**, and so forth.

For **Mars** <note>Iron</note> is dry, and very far removed from mercury—more than any other bodies—and all dryness has the character of emptiness. For this reason, our mercury, which is supremely moist, is easily received into it, by which it is led into the aforementioned softness <note>In alchemy, "softness" refers to the malleability and ductility characteristic of precious metals, as opposed to the brittleness of iron.</note>.

The form of our **Heaven** <note>original: "Cœli"; in this context, the "Heaven" refers to the quintessence or the most refined, celestial part of the alchemical substance.</note> is indeed another individual entity, in which two free operations are discerned. The first is that by which it truly converts all things placed upon it into the species under which it itself existed. This is an evident sign of an admirable **vegetative power** <note>original: "potentiæ vegetabilis"; the internal life-force believed to allow metals to "grow" or change their form like plants.</note>, by reason of which substances are transmuted into other forms. Let the **craftsman** <term>artifex</term> <note>The alchemist or practitioner of the Great Work.</note> therefore consider what he might be able to work, stripped of his own form and shaped by another, where our mercury shall be. There is also another operation equally evident: when the bodies of the **perfect metals** <note>Gold and silver, which were considered "perfect" because they do not decay in fire.</note> are ground with that form upon a **porphyry** <note>original: "porphyrium"; a hard stone slab used for grinding and mixing alchemical ingredients.</note>, both of these will be reduced into the form of an **amalgamation** <term>amalgmatio</term> <note>The process of blending mercury with another metal to form a paste.</note>, from which the mercury will be extracted through a cloth, because it exists here.

<meta>catchword: it will be made by work-</meta>

...in the **Sun** <note>original: "Sole"; refers to Gold</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: "Luna"; refers to Silver</note>, it <note>Referring to "our mercury," the vital alchemical principle.</note> moves toward that which is contained by that form, and likewise the reverse. Therefore, let them be gathered in the angle of harmony in such a way that in **our heaven** <note>original: "cœlo nostro"; the "heaven" refers to the alchemical vessel or the controlled environment within the flask where the transformation occurs.</note> the perfect bodies may be multiplied, and their Mercury can in no way resist, but rather is brought back into the form of an **amalgam** <term>amalgama: a mixture of mercury with another metal, usually resulting in a softened or liquid-like state</term> by another natural bond. In other specific substances, these operations of our heaven could also be recorded, which we omit for the sake of brevity, because what has been said is sufficient for understanding them.

The goal of this entire **mastery** <note>original: "magisterii"; the "Great Work" or the highest level of alchemical practice.</note> is that our mercury be placed in the celestial sphere; otherwise, the craftsman will labor in vain. It seems marvelous that our mercury, situated in a super-celestial place, does not retain its color, weight, quantity, and quality, even though all these things belong to its nature; but the reason for this is that it is placed under a different form than when it lay hidden within a specific body. 

Here also is the method for extracting the mercury in this artifice: namely, let the best white or red wine be placed for separation, and from it let **burning water** <note>original: "ardens aqua"; a historical term for distilled alcohol or brandy, named for its flammability.</note> be distilled, as is customary. Afterward, let it be **rectified** <term>rectificatio: the process of purifying a liquid through repeated distillation</term> four times for greater subtlety, though three times would suffice, lest the burning spirit evaporate; those who rectify it seven times have therefore erred. Let this be the sign of the purification: when **sugar** <note>original: "saccharum"</note> is added, it is burned entirely along with the water, leaving nothing behind. This material is the subject from which the **quintessence** <note>original: "essentia quinta"; the "fifth essence," a celestial and incorruptible medicinal or transformative substance.</note> is drawn, by means of the vessel called the **pelican** <note>original: "pellicanum"; a specialized circulatory distillation flask with two side arms that return the condensed liquid back to the base, named for its resemblance to a pelican pecking its breast.</note>.
